### Page 2

during Aug, resulting in 3Q26 revenues up 74% YoY to NT$843m. With the gradual ramp 
up of clients’ new platform, we expect to see sequential MoM growth ahead, with Oct/ 
Nov revenues up 17%/ 19% MoM to NT$341m/ NT$406m, driven by a rising 
contribution from the LEO satellites.

Earnings revision: We factor in UMT 3Q26 revenues and revise down earnings by 1%/ 
1%/ 1% in 2026-28E mainly on lower revenues due to the momentum of product 
delivery, while we remain positive on the rising LEO satellite business exposure ahead.

Valuation: We continue to derive our target price based on a discounted P/E, and based 
on 2029E (methodology unchanged). We continue to derive the target P/E multiple from 
the average PEG&M ratio of peers in the satellite supply chain, and we remove outliers 
(telecom operators who face competition from LEO satellite operators). Our target 
2029E discounted P/E multiple is at 37.1x (vs. previously at 37.0x) which is based on 
peers’ avg. PEG&M, and UMT’s 2029-30E avg. NI YoY and OPM, due to higher avg. OPM 
in 2029-30E at 42% (vs. previously at 41%). We apply the 37.1x multiple to 2029E EPS 
and discount it back to 2027E via a COE of 7.8% (no change). Our 12m target price is at 
NT$2,549 (vs. previously at NT$2,557), implying 61x 2027E P/E, which is above the 
company’s average +1 stv. P/E of 40x, reflecting our positive view on UMT’s product mix 
upgrade toward LEO satellites. Maintain Buy.

### Page 4

Price target risks and methodology - UMT 
Valuation: We use a discounted P/E methodology and apply a 37.1x target P/E multiple 
to UMT’s 2029E EPS, discounting it back to 2027E at a COE of 7.8% (beta 1.2x, risk-free 
rate 1.6% and market risk premium at 5.1%), which leads to our 12-month target price 
of NT$2,549. The target P/E is based on the average PEG&M ratio of peers in the 
satellite supply chain. We are Buy-rated on UMT.  
Key Risks: Slower-than-expected LEO satellite deployment; Potential competition from 
new-entrant suppliers; LEO satellite operators manufacturing in-house components.

Source: Company data, Goldman Sachs Research estimates, FactSet. Price as of 12 Oct 2026 close.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.
Not Rated (NR). The investment rating, target price and earnings estimates (where relevant) are removed pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when
Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that
has passed a Phase II clinical trial nor a license to distribute a post-Phase II drug, treatment or medical device.  Rating Suspended (RS). Goldman Sachs
Research has suspended the investment rating and price target for this stock, because there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an
investment rating or target price. The previous investment rating and target price, if any, are no longer in effect for this stock and should not be relied

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this
company.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
TSMC was founded in 1987 and is listed in Taiwan 
(2330TT) and the US (TSM/NYS).TSMC is the largest and 
global leader in integrated circuit (IC) manufacturing. As a 
build-to-order foundry, it provides a wide range of value-
add activities: IC manufacturing, mask-making, IC design 
services, turnkey solutions, and process development. We 
attribute its success to its proven, winning business model, 
unparalleled scale advantage, optimized execution, and 
technology scope and depth. 5 shares = 1 ADR.

Investment Rationale 
The ongoing semi content growth in mobile, rise of artificial 
intelligence (AI), and proliferation of Internet of Things (IoT) 
should result in sustainable upside in aggregate computing 
power globally. TSMC, as the leading contract manufacturer 
of semiconductor chips, is in a good position to capitalize. 
We are structurally positive on TSMC and expect it to 
sustain over 15% growth with rising FCF from 2026 
onwards.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co. (TSMWF / TSM) 
Our PO for TSMC is NT$3,260 per share (US$620 per ADR), based on 20x 2027E P/E. 
TSMC's valuation has traded in the range of 10-26x PE from 2017. 20x is in the mid to 
high end of the historical range, above the average P/E of 16x. We think a PE above the 
average is justified given TSMC's enhanced industry position and mid-to-high 50% 5-
year CAGR in AI should underpin a rerating

Downside risks to our price objective are: (1) greater-than-expected slowdown in global 
smartphone/consumer electronics demand, (2) Intel's potential insourcing strategy and 
ambitions in foundry service, (3) execution risks on advanced nodes, and (4) higher than 
expected tariffs.

Document 161: 9 月会合以降に日銀を取り巻く状況が変わった印象

Source PDF: all report pdf/9 月会合以降に日銀を取り巻く状況が変わった印象.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 7 日(水)

1/3 
9 月会合以降に日銀を取り巻く状況が変わった印象  
 経済調査部主席エコノミスト藤代宏一 
（℡：050-5474-6123）

・時事通信は10月６日付で「日銀が『基調的な物価上昇率』について、２％程度に到達したと
判断する方向で検討に入ることが５日、分かった」と報じた。記事内には10月29・30日開催
の金融政策決定会合で取りまとめる展望レポートで、そうした認識が記述されるとしてい
る。

・筆者はこの報道が現実になる可能性が高いと判断している。日銀は予てから基調的物価上昇
率が２％に到達する時期を「2026年度後半から2027年度」と予想してきたので（2026年度後
半にあたる）10月にそうした認識を示さないと、ハト派的な印象を与えてしまうことにな
る。

・既に日銀の政策態度は基調的物価上昇率を２％に「引き上げる」段階から、２％以上に「上
振れさせない」段階に移行している。局面が変化した理由としては、やはり原油高とそれに
対する企業の価格設定行動の積極化が大きいだろう。従来、原油高は企業収益を圧迫するこ
とで、賃上げを阻害し、個人消費の停滞に繋がってきた。そうした環境下で内需は拡大しな
いため、原油高は短期的にインフレ要因となっても、やや長い目でみるとデフレ的な色彩を
帯びていた。もっとも、2026年春先以降の原油高局面では、政府の手厚い支援もあり、企業
・日経平均株価は先行き12 ヶ月72,000 円程度で推移するだろう 
・USD/JPY は先行き12 ヶ月 155 円程度で推移するだろう 
・日銀は政策金利を12 月に 1.5％とした後、27 年末までに2.25％とするだろう 
・FED はFF 金利を、年内は4.0％で据え置くだろう

### Page 2

2/3 
は価格転嫁を積極的に進めた。企業物価指数は４月に前月比＋2.8％と即座に上昇し、前年比
伸び率は８月時点でも＋7.6％と高止まりしている。日銀短観（６月および９月調査）では１
年後の販売価格見通しが急伸した。こうした早期かつ大幅な価格転嫁の進展によって、企業
収益は傷みにくくなっている。このことは、名目賃金と名目消費が伸びやすい構図にあるこ
とを浮き彫りにしている。

・日銀が、基調的物価上昇率が２％に到達したとの認識を示すのであれば、それは利上げペー
スの加速を自ら正当化することになる。さすがに10月の利上げはビハインド・ザ・カーブに
陥ったことを認めたと市場参加者に受け止められる恐れがあるため、見送られると予想する
が、（前回利上げから約３ヶ月が経過する）12月会合では利上げが決定されるだろう。そう
なれば、３～４ヶ月に一度の利上げが標準ペースとなることが市場参加者に広く意識される
だろう。

・利上げを巡っては、経済指標に加えて「政府との距離感」を考慮する必要があり、予想は難
儀であった。しかしながら、ここ数週間で情勢は変化したように感じる。９月25日に片山さ
つき財務相が「官邸とも相談の上、私から初めて申し上げるが、先日の日米首脳会談では、
為替について、トランプ大統領から円安への懸念が示された」と発言したことを皮切りに、
同日には城内経済再生相が「アベノミクス的な意味でのリフレーション政策は、もうそうい
う局面は終わっている」とした。また10月５日に公表された９月30日の経済財政諮問会議の
議事要旨では、民間議員である若田部昌澄氏が「デフレではない状況になりつつある現状で
リフレ政策が終焉を迎えているというのは当然のことだ」との見解を示し、さらに９月の金
融政策決定会合で反対票を投じた佐藤綾野日銀政策委員は６日までに共同通信の単独インタ

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
ビューに応じて「（政策金利を）段階的に調整していく方針には賛成している」とした。９
月18日会合における「主な意見」で内閣府の出席者（おそらく城内大臣）が利上げに消極的
と読める発言をしていたことと整合しないが、それは9月18日から25日の間に政府の態度が変
化した可能性が指摘できる。

・10月会合までに円安が進む、あるいは原油高等の要因によってＦｅｄが利上げに踏み切る事
態となれば、日銀も追随する可能性があるものの、そうした状況に至らなければ、10月は
「既往の利上げ効果を見極めたい」などとして利上げを見送るだろう。

・なお、これらに鑑みて、筆者は2027年７月の審議委員人事が中立ないしはタカ派的な人選に
なると予想する。高田委員と田村委員に考え方の近い人物に白羽の矢が立ち、リフレ派が送
り込まれることはないと考えている。利上げに積極的な２名の後任人事にリフレ派と目され
る人物が浮上した場合、金融市場では長期金利上昇や円安の反応が予想される。

9 月经济数据点评：生产偏强、需求渐进修复

[Table_Author] 
 魏争 分析师  沈夏宜 分析师

6.2%升至 9.8%，我们理解部分原因是地缘局势提高能源安全和保供要
求，国内油气增储上产持续推进，为采矿业生产提供了支撑 。二是电热燃
水业增速回升。电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业增加值增长 5.6%，
较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点。从实物产量看，核电、风电和太阳能发电分别
增长 21.5%、10.6%和 19.5%。 
服务业生产明显改善，生产性服务业延续支撑，生活性服务有修复。9
月，服务业生产指数同比增长 4.8%，较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点；一是数
字信息和生产配套型服务业继续保持较快增长。 信息传输软件和信息技术
服务业、租赁和商务服务业、交通运输仓储和邮政业生产指数 分别增长
9.8%、9.5%和 5.4%。二是服务业改善广度有提升。住宿和餐饮业生产指
数增长 5.3%，服务业商务活动指数由 49.3%升至 50.2%，重新进入扩张
区间；电信广播电视及卫星传输、货币金融和保险等行业商务活动指数均
位于 55%以上1。 
投资低位企稳。9 月，固定资产投资累计同比下降 7.2%，降幅与 8 月持
平。按累计金额反推，9 月当月投资同比约下降 7.2%，较 8 月的下降
11.0%明显收窄；剔除房地产后，当月投资同比约下降 3.4%，降幅较 8
月收窄约 4.4 个百分点。季调环比增长 0.07%，是 3 月以来首次转正。 
制造业投资小幅修复，增长仍集中在少数优势领域 。9 月，制造业累计投
资下降 2.1%，降幅较 1—8 月收窄 0.2 个百分点。结构上看，一是正增行
业仍主要集中在装备和新兴制造领域。国家统计局公布的 13 个制造业行
业中，只有 5 个保持正增长，其中铁路船舶航空航天、计算机通信电子设
备制造业投资分别增长 15.8%和 9.6%，纺织、电气机械和通用设备制造
业分别增长 4.2%、1.2%和 1.1%。高技术产业投资增长 4.9%，其中信息
服务、航空航天器及设备、电子及通信设备制造业投资分别增长 20.2%、
12.5%和 7.6%。二是上游原材料和下游消费制造业投资仍较弱。农副食
品加工、食品制造、化工、医药、有色金属冶炼、金属制品、专用设备和
汽车制造业投资分别下降 2.5%、10.3%、7.5%、8.1%、5.1%、10.1%、
8.9%和 6.3%。 
地产继续承压，价格端修复难抵销售走弱。9 月，地产投资累计同比下降
20.6%，降幅较 8 月扩大 0.7 个百分点；按累计金额反推，9 月当月投资
下降约 26.3%，降幅较 8 月扩大约 0.8 个百分点。结构上看，一是地产销
售再次走弱，约束投资预期。 商品房销售面积和销售额累计分别下降
13.2%和 13.9%，降幅较 1—8 月扩大 1.1 和 0.9 个百分点；按累计金额反
推，9 月当月销售面积和销售额分别下降约 20.7%和 20.1%，较 8 月的下
降 14.8%和 12.1%明显走弱。东部地区销售额降幅由 12.6%扩大至
13.4%。30 大中城市商品房成交面积同比下降 10.9%，较 8 月扩大约 7.5
个百分点，其中一线城市增速由 18.8%降至 2.4%，二线城市降幅由
15.8%扩大至 22.7%，三线城市降幅基本持平。二是供给和资金端仍然偏
弱。新开工面积累计下降 24.9%，降幅较 8 月扩大 0.1 个百分点，竣工面
积降幅由 23.7%扩大至 25.1%。房企到位资金下降 21.6%，降幅扩大 0.6

### Page 3

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 3 
个百分点，其中，自筹资金降幅由 20.0%扩大至 21.4%；国内贷款和个人
按揭贷款降幅则分别小幅收窄至 33.1%和 22.1%。三是价格端有积极变
化。9 月，70 城新房和二手房价格同比分别下降 3.0%和 4.8%，降幅均较
8 月收窄 0.3 个百分点；环比看，新房降幅由 0.2%收窄至 0.1%，二手房
下降 0.3%，与上月持平。一线城市新房和二手房价格分别上涨 0.1%和
0.2%，二线城市新房价格由下降转为持平、二手房降幅收窄。 
基建投资继续放缓，项目端景气 有改善迹象。9 月，基建投资累计同比下
降 4.2%，降幅较 8 月扩大 0.2 个百分点。一方面，部分传统基建领域仍
然偏弱。交通运输仓储和邮政业投资增长 1.9%，较前期回落 0.2 个百分
点；水利环境和公共设施管理业投资下降 12.8%，降幅扩大 1.8 个百分
点，其中水利管理和公共设施管理业均下降 12%以上。电热燃水投资下降
6.8%，降幅较前期小幅收窄。另一方面，项目储备和部分结构性领域出现
积极信号，但向施工和实物工作量的传导仍不充分。9 月，建筑业商务活
动指数由 46.9%升至 50.3%，从业人员和业务活动预期指数也有所提高。
信息传输、航空运输和水上运输投资分别增长 30.8%、13.8%和 13.5%，
交通、能源、数据等“六张网”相关领域仍有支撑。 建筑业新订单指数由
42.4%升至 45.7%，尚未回到扩张区间，指向前端项目需求有改善，但向
施工和实物工作量的传导仍不充分。 
消费是内需端改善最明显的分项，商品消费仍受汽车拖累 。9 月，社零同
比增长 1.1%，较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点，环比增长 0.35%，剔除汽车后
社零同比增长 3.9%，较 8 月加快 1.4 个百分点。结构上看，商品和餐饮
消费同步恢复。9 月，商品零售额增长 1.0%，较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点；
餐饮收入增长 2.1%，提高 1.0 个百分点。从更广义的消费口径看，服务
消费仍是消费的重要支撑。三季度，社会消费商品和服务零售总额增长
2.6%，其中服务零售额增长 5.0%，明显高于商品零售额的 1.0%；网上
服务零售额增长 5.3%。通讯信息、旅游咨询租赁和文体休闲服务零售额
保持较快增长。商品消费方面，必选消费和通讯器材维持韧性，汽车是主
要拖累。服装、化妆品、日用品、家电和中西药品零售额分别增长
1.2%、5.4%、4.1%、1.9%和 5.0%，通讯器材类增长 31.1%。汽车零售
额下降 21.2%，降幅较 8 月扩大 2.7 个百分点；家具、建筑装潢材料仍分
别下降 3.0%和 9.2%，降幅较 8 月有所收窄。 
展望后续，四季度经济有望延续生产较强、需求渐进修复的格局，未来增
量支撑主要来自两条线索。总体看，三季度实际增长边际企稳，优势制造
业、外需和服务消费构成主要支撑。往后看，增量支撑线索有两方面。一
方面，以“六张网”和 8000 亿元新型政策性金融工具为抓手，加快资金
投放和项目开工。9 月以来三家政策性银行投放的新型政策性金融工具资
金已经超过 1000 亿元2，建筑业 PMI 和新订单指数同步回升，指向政策
正在向项目端传导。不过，从补充资本金、落实配套融资到形成施工和实
物工作量仍存在时滞，基建对传统建筑链的拉动或更多在四季度后半段逐
步显现。另一方面，服务消费仍是扩大内需更具确定性的方向。9 月多部
门出台金融支持服务业扩能提质政策，叠加扩大消费“十五五”规划对养

宏观经济点评

老、托育、文旅、健康等领域的部署，服务消费有望继续快于商品消费。

风险提示：外需边际走弱、地缘政治超预期恶化、AI 产业链降温超预期、
政策资金投放节奏不及预期。

### Page 7

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 7 
1. GDP：实际增长边际回升 
实际 GDP 边际回升， 名义增长继续快于实际增长。三季度经济增长呈现三个特征，
一是实际增长较二季度有改善。 三季度实际GDP 同比增长 4.5%， 高于二季度的4.3%，
但仍略低于上半年平均水平；前三季度 GDP 累计增长 4.6%，较上半年回落 0.1 个百分
点。分产业看，实际 GDP 回升主要来自第二产业改善。第二产业增加值增速由二季度
的 3.0%回升至 3.4%， 第三产业由5.1%小幅升至 5.2%， 第一产业则由3.7%回落至 3.6%。
二是名义 GDP 继续快于实际 GDP，第二产业价格修复仍是重要支撑。 根据现价计算，
三季度名义 GDP 同比增长约 6.0%，较二季度提高 0.1 个百分点，高于实际 GDP 增速
1.5 个百分点。按名义与实际增速差近似估算，三季度 GDP 平减指数约为 1.5%，较二
季度的 1.6%略有回落， 指向整体价格修复仍在延续， 但没有进一步加快。 结构上， 第二
产业价格为主要支撑。二季度，第一产业平减指数由-3.3%回升至-1.7%，第三产业则由
1.3%回落至 0.7%， 第二产业平减指数约3.0%升至 3.6%， 其中工业由3.8%上升至 4.4%，
其表现与国际能源价格上涨以及 AI 产业链、 有色和电子设备价格较强基本一致。三是内
需端对经济增长的支撑边际回落，其中投资为最大拖累，外需端支撑边际增强。 支出法
看， 前三季度最终消费支出对经济增长的贡献率为45.7%， 较上半年回落0.3 个百分点；
资本形成总额贡献率为 28.3%，较上半年回落 7.8 个百分点；货物和服务净出口贡献率
为 26.1%，较上半年提升 8.2 个百分点。

### Page 8

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 8 
2. 生产：工业继续回升 
工业生产继续回升，采矿业和电热燃水业改善显著。9 月，规上工业增加值同比增
长 5.5%，较 8 月提高 0.3 个百分点，环比增长 0.6%。总量上看，出口对工业生产的拉
动延续。9 月，出口交货值同比增长 17.0%，较 8 月提高 5.9 个百分点。结构上看，工
业生产回升有两方面原因。一方面，制造业增加值维持韧性，高增长仍集中在装备制造
和高技术制造领域。9 月，制造业增加值增长 6.0%，较 8 月小幅回落 0.1 个百分点，但
仍高于整体工业增速。其中，高技术制造业增长 15.3%；金属制品、通用设备、专用设
备、 铁路船舶航空航天、 电气机械和计算机通信电子设备制造业分别增长 8.2%、8.5%、
12.1%、11.7%、8.1%和 16.4%。下游消费制造业总体保持正增。农副食品加工、食品
制造、 酒饮料和精制茶、 纺织、 医药和汽车制造业分别增长4.1%、7.3%、5.5%、3.2%、
4.9%和 6.7%。另一方面，拖累项增加值边际修复。一是采矿业由负转正，对工业生产
的拖累明显减轻。9 月，采矿业增加值由下降 1.4%转为增长 1.3%，其中煤炭开采和洗
选业降幅由 5.6%收窄至 3.3%，石油和天然气开采业增速由 6.2%升至 9.8%，我们理解
部分原因是地缘局势提高能源安全和保供要求，国内油气 增储上产持续推进，为采矿业
生产提供了支撑。二是电热燃水业增速回升。电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业增加
值增长 5.6%，较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点。 从实物产量看， 核电、 风电和太阳能发电分别
增长 21.5%、10.6%和 19.5%。 
服务业生产明显改善，生产性服务业延续支撑，生活性服务有修复。 9 月，服务业
生产指数同比增长 4.8%，较8 月提高 0.7 个百分点； 一是数字信息和生产配套型服务业
继续保持较快增长。信息传输软件和信息技术服务业、租赁和商务服务业、交通运输仓
储和邮政业生产指数分别增长 9.8%、9.5%和 5.4%。二是服务业改善广度有提升。 住宿
和餐饮业生产指数增长 5.3%，服务业商务活动指数由 49.3%升至 50.2%，重新进入扩
张区间； 电信广播电视及卫星传输、 货币金融和保险等行业商务活动指数均位于55%以
上。 
图5 采矿业规上增加值显著回升（%）

图6 规上工业增加值环比高于季节性（%）

3. 投资：延续回落 
投资低位企稳。9 月， 固定资产投资累计同比下降7.2%， 降幅与 8 月持平。按累计
金额反推，9 月当月投资同比约下降 7.2%，较 8 月的下降 11.0%明显收窄； 剔除房地产
后， 当月投资同比约下降3.4%， 降幅较8 月收窄约 4.4 个百分点。 季调环比增长0.07%，
是 3 月以来首次转正。 
图8 固定资产投资低位企稳（%）

### Page 10

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 10 
制造业投资小幅修复， 增长仍集中在少数优势领域。9 月， 制造业累计投资下降2.1%，
降幅较 1—8 月收窄 0.2 个百分点。 结构上看， 一是正增行业仍主要集中在装备和新兴制
造领域。国家统计局公布的 13 个制造业行业中，只有 5 个保持正增长，其中铁路船舶
航空航天、 计算机通信电子设备制造业投资分别增长15.8%和 9.6%， 纺织、 电气机械和
通用设备制造业分别增长 4.2%、1.2%和 1.1%。高技术产业投资增长 4.9%，其中信息
服务、 航空航天器及设备、 电子及通信设备制造业投资分别增长20.2%、12.5%和 7.6%。
二是上游原材料和下游消费制造业投资仍较弱。 农副食品加工、 食品制造、 化工、 医药、
有色金属冶炼、 金属制品、 专用设备和汽车制造业投资分别下降2.5%、10.3%、7.5%、
8.1%、5.1%、10.1%、8.9%和 6.3%。

4. 消费：改善相对明显，服务强于商品 
消费是内需端改善最明显的分项， 商品消费仍受汽车拖累。9 月， 社零同比增长1.1%，
较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点，环比增长 0.35%，剔除汽车后社零同比增长 3.9%，较 8 月
加快 1.4 个百分点。 结构上看， 商品和餐饮消费同步恢复。9 月， 商品零售额增长1.0%，
较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点； 餐饮收入增长2.1%， 提高1.0 个百分点。 从更广义的消费口
径看， 服务消费仍是消费的重要支撑。 三季度， 社会消费商品和服务零售总额增长2.6%，
其中服务零售额增长 5.0%， 明显高于商品零售额的 1.0%； 网上服务零售额增长5.3%。
通讯信息、旅游咨询租赁和文体休闲服务零售额保持较快增长。商品消费方面，必选消
费和通讯器材维持韧性，汽车是主要拖累。服装、化妆品、日用品、家电和中西药品零
售额分别增长 1.2%、5.4%、4.1%、1.9%和 5.0%，通讯器材类增长 31.1%。汽车零售
额下降 21.2%， 降幅较8 月扩大 2.7 个百分点； 家具、 建筑装潢材料仍分别下降3.0%和
9.2%，降幅较 8 月有所收窄。 
图15 9 月社零增速边际修复（%）

图16 餐饮、商品消费均回升（%）

5. 展望：有望延续生产较强、内需修复格局 
展望后续，四季度经济有望延续生产较强、需求渐进修复的格局，未来增量支撑主
要来自两条线索。总体看，三季度实际增长边际企稳，优势制造业、外需和服务消费构
成主要支撑。 往后看， 增量支撑线索有两方面。 一方面， 以 “六张网” 和8000 亿元新型
政策性金融工具为抓手，加快资金投放和项目开工。9 月以来三家政策性银行投放的新
型政策性金融工具资金已经超过 1000 亿元，建筑业 PMI 和新订单指数同步回升，指向
政策正在向项目端传导。不过，从补充资本金、落实配套融资到形成施工和实物工作量
仍存在时滞，基建对传统建筑链的拉动或更多在四季度后半段逐步显现。另一方面，服
务消费仍是扩大内需更具确定性的方向。9 月多部门出台金融支持服务业扩能提质政策，
叠加扩大消费“十五五”规划对养老、托育、文旅、健康等领域的部署，服务消费有望
继续快于商品消费。 
风险提示 
外需边际走弱、地缘政治超预期恶化、AI 产业链降温超预期、政策资金投放节奏不
及预期。

投资评级说明 
投资建议的评级标准  评级 说明 
评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价 （或行业
指数） 相对同期基准指数的相对市场表现。 其中A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准； 新三板市场以三板成指 （针
对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对同期基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对同期基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

3Q26 전사 예상 영업이익 100조원 
DS부문 예상 영업이익은 100.0조원(OPM 67.4%, YoY +1538.5%, QoQ +14.5%) 으로, 
달러환율 1,380원기준, 메모리와 비메모리 사업부문 예상 영업손익은 각각 102.0조원,    
-2.0조원으로 추정한다. 1) 메모리반도체 가격 상승세가 이어지는 가운데, HBM4 판매 
본격화에 따른 영향을 고려하면 DRAM/NAND 분기 평균 가격 상승폭은 각각 +18.0%, 
+16.0%으로 추정하고, 2)비메모리반도체 사업 적자는 지속될 전망이기 때문이다. 다만 
특별 성과급 지급에 따른 충당금 영향을 제외하고 선단공정 중심의 파운드리 가동률을 
고려하면 적자폭은 유의미하게 감소한 것으로 파악된다는 점이 긍정적이다.   
SDC부문 예상 영업이익은 1.1조원(OPM 13.0%, YoY -9.6%, QoQ +54.9%)으로, 1)국내 
주력 고객사향 폴더블 스마트폰 판매 호조가 사업부의 외형 성장에 긍정적으로 작용했을 
것으로 추정됨에도 불구하고, 2) 8.6세대 IT OLED 패널 양산 개시에 따른 감가상각비(약 
0.3조원 수준)와 북미 고객 중심의 판가 인하 압박 영향으로 전년동기대비 수익성은 약 
2%pt 훼손이 불가피할 전망이다. DX부문 예상 영업손익은 -1.1조원(YoY 적자전환, QoQ 
적자지속)으로, 메모리반도체를 포함한 IT 부품 가격 부담이 전분기대비 확대되고 있기 
때문이다. MX와 VD/DA사업부 예상 손익은 각각 -0.9조원, -0.2조원으로 추정한다.

투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 63만원으로 상향 조정  
메모리반도체의 가격 상승폭이 다소 둔화되겠지만, 2027~2028년에도 타이트한 수급 
환경이 이어질 전망이라는 점을 주목한다. 1)시장은 신규 증설과 HBM 관련 노이즈로 인한 
2028년 피크아웃 가능성을 우려하고 있으나, HBM 의 범용 DRAM 생산능력 잠식과 AI 
추론 확산에 따른 고용량 메모리 수요를 감안하면 공급 제약의 해소 시점은 예상보다 
늦어질 가능성이 높기 때문이다. 뿐만 아니라, 2)메모리 업사이클이 장기화될 경우 동사 
FCF는 과거대비 구조적으로 확대되어 보다 친화적인 주주환원 정책(2027~2029년, 
2027년 1월 예정)을 제시할 가능성도 배제할 수 없기 때문이다. 동사 2027년 연간 실적 
추정치 상향 조정에 따라 목표주가를 63만원(2027년 BPS * Target PBR 3.5배 적용)으로 
상향 조정한다. 피크아웃 우려에 따른 주가 조정은 중장기 관점에서 매수 기회다.

### Page 1

美股盘初⾛⾼，标普涨 1% ，道指涨 0.5% ，纳指涨 1.3% ，纳斯达克 100 指数涨 1.4% ，费城
半导体指数⽇内涨 3% 。英伟达涨 3.11% ，博通涨 2.83% ，美光涨 3.47% ，英特尔涨
3.23% 。巴西在美股上市的公司暴跌， XP 跌超 52% ， PAGSEGURO DIGITAL 跌超 33% ，
伊塔乌联合银⾏跌超 23% ，巴西⽯油跌超 17% 。
摘要：
科技巨头财报季正式拉开帷幕，全球市场在多重不确定性交织下谨慎观望。
美股盘初⾛⾼，标普涨 1% ，道指涨 0.5% ，纳指涨 1.3% ，纳斯达克 100 指数涨 1.4% ，银⾏指数
涨 1.4% 。
费城半导体指数⽇内涨 3% 。存储芯⽚指数涨 3.2% ， AI 软件先驱指数涨 1.5% 、 AI 赢家指数涨
2.7% 。标普半导体设备指数涨 3% 。
英伟达涨 3.11% ，博通涨 2.83% ，美光涨 3.47% ，英特尔涨 3.23% ， AMD 涨 2.34% ，⾼通涨
2.09% ，台积电涨 2.04% ，阿斯⻨ ADR 涨 2.46% 。 SK 海⼒⼠涨 3.3% ，公司据报筹备 50 万亿⾄ 60

万亿韩元股东回报计划。 Meta 涨 0.3% ，公司寻求最多 950 亿美元股权融资以⽀持 AI ⽀出。
巴西在美股上市的公司暴跌， XP 跌超 52% ， PAGSEGURO DIGITAL 跌超 33% ，伊塔乌联合银⾏
跌超 23% ，巴西⽯油跌超 17% 。
巴西⼤选结果引发市场震荡。卢拉（ Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva ）在周⽇决选中意外击败右翼候选
⼈ Flávio Bolsonaro ，在⽇本上市的巴西 ETF 盘中⼀度暴跌 16% ， CME 外汇衍⽣品合约显⽰雷亚
尔兑美元汇率隐含跌幅逾 10% 。
本周，微软、 Alphabet 及亚⻢逊等科技巨头相继发布财报， AI 基础设施投⼊能否持续将接受市场
检验。与此同时，美联储和⽇本央⾏均将公布利率决议，全球债市及汇市料将因此波动加剧。
AT Global Markets 驻悉尼⾸席市场分析师 Nick Twidale 在研报中指出，市场焦点将 " 从数周来主
导⾛势的地缘政治头条，重新转向央⾏政策与利率⾛向 " 。

美股盘初⾛⾼，标普涨 1% ，道指涨 0.5% ，纳指涨 1.3% ，纳斯达克 100 指数涨 1.4% ，费城半
导体指数⽇内涨 3% 。
韩国⾸尔综指收跌 1.45% ，报 5469.59 点，⽇经 225 指数收跌 0.8% ，报 62189.65 点，东证指
数逆势收涨 0.2% ，报 3972.98 点。
欧股开盘涨跌不⼀，  欧洲斯托克 50 指数开盘涨 0.07% ，德国 DAX 指数涨 0.28% ，英国富时
100 指数跌 0.17% ，法国 CAC 40 指数涨 0.60% 。
美国 10 年期国债收益率⼩幅下⾏ 3 个基点⾄ 4.97% ，美债整体⾛强。
⽇本 10 年期国债收益率下跌 2 个基点，⾄ 2.780%
布伦特原油从六个⽉⾼位回落，跌幅 1.5% ，报约 114.95 美元 / 桶
现货⻩⾦上涨 0.3% ，⾄每盎司 4008.18 美元

### Page 2

Anthropic 提交 IPO 申请，美股 AI 交易情绪修复
美股盘初⾛⾼，标普涨 1% ，道指涨 0.5% ，纳指涨 1.3% ，纳斯达克 100 指数涨 1.4% ，银⾏指数
涨 1.4% 。
Anthropic 正式提交 IPO 申请，公司上半年营收 163 亿美元，净亏损却达 807 亿美元；第三季度，
79% 的营收来⾃前五⼤客⼾以外的客⼾群体，客⼾结构的分散度进⼀步提升。同时公司称未来
可能⽆法实现盈利，警⽰⼈类 “ ⽣存危机 ” 。
费城半导体指数⽇内涨 3% 。存储芯⽚指数涨 3.2% ， AI 软件先驱指数涨 1.5% 、 AI 赢家指数涨
2.7% 。标普半导体设备指数涨 3% 。
英伟达涨 3.11% ，博通涨 2.83% ，美光涨 3.47% ，英特尔涨 3.23% ， AMD 涨 2.34% ，⾼通涨
2.09% ，台积电涨 2.04% ，阿斯⻨ ADR 涨 2.46% 。
SK 海⼒⼠涨 3.3% ，公司据报筹备 50 万亿⾄ 60 万亿韩元股东回报计划。 Meta 涨 0.3% ，公司寻求

最多 950 亿美元股权融资以⽀持 AI ⽀出。
央⾏决议与财报周双重考验在即
本周市场⽇程密集。 AT Global Markets 驻悉尼⾸席市场分析师 Nick Twidale 在研报中指出，市场
焦点将 " 从数周来主导⾛势的地缘政治头条，重新转向央⾏政策与利率⾛向 " 。
美联储和⽇本央⾏的政策决定备受瞩⽬，⼆者均⾯临通胀顽固、能源成本上升及全球债券收益
率⾼企的压⼒。美国 10 年期国债收益率⼩幅下⾏ 3 个基点⾄ 4.97% ，美债整体⾛强。
新加坡星展银⾏⾼级利率策略师 Eugene Leow 表⽰， "AI 疑虑、增⻓放缓⻛险、欧洲及美国中期
选举带来的政治不确定性，将使市场对避险资产的需求维持在⾼位。在当前绝对收益率⽔平
下，美债应可发挥良好的避险对冲功能。 "

Document 241: Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: getting the balance right
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
10:46 October 16 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s key data release this week was the September Labour Force Survey. Employment surprised
to the downside, -39.3k. However, a 0.2ppt decline in labour force participation from August’s near-
historic high largely offset. The unemployment rate therefore held at 4.7% after August was revised up.

Residual seasonality may be in the mix. Regardless, the take home point from the update is that the

### Page 2

labour market is continuing to ease as employment growth runs below working-age population growth.
We expect the unemployment rate to grind higher into 2027 as tighter financial conditions and cost-of-
living pressures restrict activity growth to a below-trend pace. Although, our forecast peak is near at
4.8%, due by Q2 2027.
This update on the labour market complicates the communication challenge for the RBA. The
September minutes were firmly on the hawkish side, with the discussion focussing on materialising

upside risks to inflation. But, with the unemployment rate now through the RBA’s NAIRU assumption of
4.6%, Governor Bullock’s “a little tight” descriptor for the labour market is becoming harder to defend.
Still, with a clear focus on inflation risks – which also led us to revise up our near-term CPI forecasts in
the October Market Outlook – we still expect a 25bp rate hike at the November meeting.
Over in the US, the headline CPI rose 0.6% as expected in September, taking the annual rate up to

3.7%yr. Energy prices were a key contributor (5.2%, 20.4%yr), while core inflation was a touch above
consensus in the month at 0.3% (2.6%yr). Within the core components, core goods prices gained 0.3%
after being broadly unchanged over the past 6 months (0.7%yr). Core services meanwhile rose 0.4%
(3.1%yr) on a 0.4% increase in shelter (3.2%yr), a 0.6% gain for transport services and a 0.3%
increase in medical care services (2.7%yr and 2.5%yr respectively). Further, the subsequent release of

the PPI pointed to continued upstream price pressures, with the headline and core measures up
6.3%yr and 5.0%yr respectively. The latest Beige Book provided colour on these developments, firms
across multiple districts citing tariffs, insurance and commodity prices as ongoing sources of cost
pressure. Guiding on the risks, many respondents expect further price increases as contracts/hedges
roll off.
Given their telegraphed concerns over inflation, there is enough signal in these reports to warrant a

follow-up hike at the October FOMC meeting. Though it seems Committee members believe they are
near the end of the tightening cycle and so have time on their side. This week, Daly and Bowman both
showed patience in assessing the trend, with no clear bias towards an October or December decision.
Williams stated he believes one more hike would prove sufficient in this cycle given known information.
And Waller also showed confidence, highlighting that elevated long-term yields are doing some of the

FOMC’s work, restricting demand in interest rate sensitive sectors. We hold a similar view, anticipating
that one more hike by year end and a little time will bring inflation near the 2.0%yr medium-term target.
However, as discussed in this month’s Market Outlook, capacity pressures are likely to see inflation
consistently above target, rather than below, in coming years – particularly if, as we suspect, capacity
in the housing sector remains tight. This will mean the FOMC have to remain modestly restrictive over

the forecast horizon and will also bias up long-term yields.
In Asia, there was one release that stood out, China’s trade account. At USD133.5bn, the September
result was a whisker inside January 2025’s record high of USD137.9bn. More impressively, throughout
2025 and 2026 year-to-date the surplus has now averaged USD100.9bn, almost 3 times the average of
2019, prior to the pandemic. The geographic breadth of export growth is as impressive as the headline

annual gain of 36.3%yr, even more so given the ongoing strength in Chinese investment in
neighbouring nations’ production chains. In time, both trade and income receipts from offshore
investment should buoy the renminbi, although for now continued outbound foreign investment should
moderate the pace of gains against other nations in the region, and likely versus the US dollar.

### Page 3

Related articles
Another key trend assessed in October’s Market Outlook is the evolution of producer prices across
Asia, and the likelihood of the disinflationary benefit the west has received in recent decades
continuing. Historically, labour cost arbitrage was one of the key drivers of declining goods prices.
While still possible, we expect the next phase of manufacturing development to instead be driven by
capital, technology and energy. Many end users and the manufacturers undertaking production are

also increasingly prioritising resilience, security and specialisation over cost minimisation. It is therefore
more probably future supply-chain developments will help contain rising costs through productivity than
drive sustained price declines for the goods bought by consumers and businesses.

Document 257: ECB caught in the French drama

Source PDF: all report pdf/ECB caught in the French drama.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

The European Central Bank meeting on 29 October is not
expected to result in an interest rate hike. Instead, the French
crisis is increasingly eroding the need for additional rate hikes
Carsten Brzeski
With the surge in bond yields on the back of the crisis in France, we expect the
ECB to remain on hold at next week's meeting
Back in September, the ECB surprised markets with a more hawkish
message, at least between the lines. With headline inﬂation higher

for longer and core inﬂation expected to increase in 2027, the stage
seemed set for at least one more rate hike at the December
meeting. Some even speculated about a back-to-back increase in
October.
One month later, and after a severe ﬁscal and political crisis in
France, the ECB is unlikely to repeat the hawkish tone at next week’s
meeting. At face value, macro developments since the last meeting
would still justify another rate hike. Headline inﬂation increased

again in September and most leading indicators point to continued
resilience in the eurozone economy. However, the surge in bond
Cookie settings

### Page 2

yields on the back of the French crisis is already imposing a new
restrictive monetary policy stance. In fact, the surge in German
bond yields since the September meeting has already had roughly
the same impact on growth and inﬂation as an additional 25bp
rate hike. For France, it's closer to a 50bp hike. Or in other words, the
bond market has taken over the ECB’s job.
All of this means that the ECB is very likely to stay on hold next
week and will delay any next rate hike decision to December. This is

when new staﬀ projections will be available and when there will
also be more insights into whether higher energy prices will
eventually bring knock-on eﬀects on the rest of the economy or
not. In fact, there are three main parameters that, in our view, will
determine the ECB’s path going forward: i) the length and intensity
of the war in the Middle East. The longer oil prices remain elevated,
the higher the probability of more rate hikes; ii) the knock-on eﬀects

of higher energy prices. So far, there has hardly been any pass-
through of higher energy prices in the rest of the economy. If this
remains the case, the need for further rate hikes drops. If it doesn’t,
the likelihood of new rate hikes goes up. iii) France. Any solution to
the current French ﬁscal woes could bring some relief to bond
markets and allow the ECB to continue hiking rates. Without any
solution, bond markets have taken over the ECB’s role, making rate

hikes less likely (and less needed).
Because it's France
Even if France does not play a role in the ECB’s policy statement
next week, it will be an important element at the press conference.
Political and ﬁscal concerns in France, and the resulting widening in
French spreads, have raised questions about whether the ECB could
intervene through the Transmission Protection Instrument (TPI),
which allows it to purchase securities issued by member states

whose ﬁnancing conditions have deteriorated for reasons not
justiﬁed by country-speciﬁc fundamentals. However, before
activating it, the Governing Council considers a cumulative set of
criteria to determine whether the countries concerned are pursuing
sound and sustainable ﬁscal and macroeconomic policies. In
practice, this includes compliance with the EU ﬁscal framework,

### Page 3

which would, at a minimum, require the proposed French budget to
be fully adopted.
The ECB has nevertheless left itself some discretion, stating that the
intervention criteria “will be dynamically adjusted to the unfolding
risks and conditions to be addressed”. This brings back memories of
the famous “because it’s France” by former European Commission
President Jean-Claude Juncker. Generally speaking, the ECB will be
in a diﬃcult position: intervening too quickly in support of France

may be viewed unfavourably by other member states and
interpreted as monetary ﬁnancing of the debt of a country
unwilling to follow the rules. That argues strongly against early
intervention. Also, don’t forget that the leadership changes at the
ECB over the next months (Schnabel, Lane, Lagarde) could also
aﬀect how the ECB views TPI.
Given the challenges of justifying the activation of TPI at present, we
expect the ECB to adopt a three-pronged approach to address the

French crisis.
1. The ﬁrst line of defence is a more dovish approach to policy
rates. As bond markets are currently taking over the ECB’s job
of inserting a more restrictive monetary stance on the
economy, there is less need for the ECB to hike rates
aggressively, or to hike rates at all. In fact, the ECB has already
started to prepare for this option, as ECB president Christine
Lagarde recently noted that “while growth has been resilient,
since our last meeting long-term interest rates have risen

notably, which will slow growth and reduce pass-through by
more than projected in our September exercise".
2. The second line of defence would be a covert return to bond
purchases. The ECB could instead pause quantitative tightening
temporarily and reinvest maturing bonds in its portfolio
'ﬂexibly', sending a positive signal to bond markets. This had
already happened during the Covid period.
3. Last but not least, should contagion in eurozone bond markets

intensify, we expect the ECB to eventually trigger the TPI. As
long as France remains non-compliant with the European ﬁscal

All in all, next week’s ECB meeting looks set to be uneventful on

Document 275: First Impressions_ NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change of OCR call (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change of OCR call (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change
of OCR call
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:19 October 22 2026 Share
Consumer prices rose 0.7% in the September quarter and 3.7% y/y. Core inflation
pressures are elevated and market pricing is primed for a 25bp OCR hike next week, which
we now expect.
Consumers Price Index, September quarter 2026 – key stats

### Page 2

Headline inflation
Quarterly change: +0.7% (prev: +1.5%)
Westpac forecast: +0.7%
RBNZ: +0.8%
Market median: +0.8%, range +0.5% to +1.2%

Annual change: +3.7% (prev: +4.1%)
Westpac forecast: +3.7%, RBNZ: +3.9%, Market: +3.8%

Non-tradables
Quarterly change: +1.0% (prev: +0.6%)
Westpac forecast: +1.3%
Annual change: +3.3% (prev: +3.4%)

Tradables
Quarterly change: +0.2% (prev: +2.7%)
Westpac forecast: -0.2%
Annual change: +4.3% (prev: +4.9%)

Consumer prices rose 0.7% in the September quarter. That saw the annual inflation rate easing to
3.7%, down from 4.1% in the year to June. 
Today’s result was in line with our forecast, but lower than the RBNZ’s forecast for 3.9% annual
inflation. 
However, under the surface, the details were more concerning. Measure of core inflation have taken a
step higher, with several closely watched measures now at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s
target band. 
We suspect that today’s result will leave the market continuing to price a strong chance of another
OCR hike at next Wednesday’s policy review. In that light, we think the RBNZ will regard this as the
path of least resistance, and we now expect that they will deliver (previously we expected that they
would pause and assess, before hiking at the December MPS).

What contributed to inflation in the September quarter?
Underpinning September’s 0.7% rise in consumer prices were some big price movements in both
directions. 
The largest move in prices this quarter was actually a sharp fall in fuel costs. Fuel prices aren’t low by
any stretch of the imagination. However, average prices for both petrol and diesel over the September
quarter were lower than in the June quarter when prices rocketed higher in the wake of the Middle East

### Page 3

conflict. In terms of specifics, the average price for 91 unleaded was 6.5% lower in September than in
the June quarter, while diesel prices were close to 16% lower. Those moves took 0.4ppts off inflation
this quarter. 
While prices at the pump weren’t as high as they were in the June quarter, fuel costs are still painfully
high. And that’s been bleeding through into higher prices for other goods and services. Of note, the
September quarter saw large increases in passenger transport costs, with international airfares up 14%
and domestic airfares up nearly 8%. 
The September quarter also saw the annual increases in local council rates (up 6.2%) and charges for
services like water supply (up 7.5%). That continues the run of large increases seen in recent years.
Lastly, the past few months saw a 1.0% rise in food prices, largely as a result of the seasonal rise in
prices for fresh vegetables. 
On the downside, we’ve seen continued softness in housing rents, which are the largest component of
the CPI, accounting for 11.2% of the basket. With low population growth and ample supply, rents were
flat in the September quarter and have effectively been flat since the start of this year. In annual terms,
rental inflation is the lowest it’s been in more than two decades, with regions like Wellington seeing
outright falls.

Annual and core inflation 
The annual inflation rate dropped back to 3.7% in the September quarter, down from 4.1% in the year
to June and the highest level in two years.
Looking under the surface, prices in the domestically oriented non-tradables group rose 1.0% over the
quarter, less than expected. That saw annual non-tradables inflation nudging down to 3.3% (from 3.4%
in the year to June). 
Non-tradables inflation has continued to run at above-average rates over the past year. That’s despite
the softness in economic growth and the labour market. In large part, that’s due to the continued
sizeable increases in administered prices, such as electricity charges which rose 9.3% over the past
year. 
However, prices in other areas have also been firm. Non-tradables excluding housing and utilities costs
(which also omits local council rates) was 3.4% for the year. In seasonally adjusted terms, quarterly
non-tradables inflation has been tracking around 0.8% for most of the past year. It was noted that in
sectors like construction, higher materials costs are adding to prices. Feedback from business we've
heard indicates that those higher materials costs are likely related to high oil prices. 
On the imported front, lower fuel costs saw tradable prices rising by just 0.2% over the September
quarter (though above our forecast for a small fall). That saw annual tradables inflation dropping back
to 4.3%, down sharply from the 4.9% rise in the year to June. 
While fuel costs have been a key driver for tradables inflation, under the surface, other imported
inflation pressures are looking firm. Excluding food and fuel costs, tradables prices were up 2.7% over
the past year. That’s the highest level in three years. 
With big swings in fuel costs, and concern that this is spilling over into operating costs more generally,
a key focus for the RBNZ is what’s been happening to measures of core inflation (which smooth
through quarter-to-quarter swings in individual prices and instead track the underlying trend in

### Page 4

Related articles
inflation). 
On this front we did some see worrying signs. Several closely watched measures of core inflation have
picked up, and now sit at the top of (or slightly above) the RBNZ target band. In terms of specifics, we
saw: 
Reinforcing this picture of firm underlying inflation pressures, a growing number of goods have been
increasing in price. That could be an early indication that businesses, even those with a limited
exposure to fuel costs, are starting to factor in expectations of continued high inflation when setting
prices.

Outlook 
While today’s result was lower than the RBNZ assumed in their September MPS projections, the
firmness in core inflation will give the RBNZ pause for thought ahead of their policy review on 28
October. Furthermore, with a sharp rise in petrol prices in recent weeks, September’s easing in annual
inflation is likely to be just a temporary reprieve. We expect headline inflation to reaccelerate back over
4% before the end of this year, and it’s set to remain above the RBNZ’s current forecast for most of
2027. 
Market pricing and views have coalesced around an October hike which we now suspect the MPC will
see as the path of least resistance. Core inflation measures are now close to or above 3%, which will
add fuel to arguments from the MPC hawks. We will have more detail in our MPR Preview later today,
including on the implications for the shape of the tightening cycle moving forward.

Kimi K3.1 Pro 是 K3 系列的⾸个模型，⽀持百万 Token 上下⽂窗⼝，代码、 Agent 和知识类
基准⽅⾯达到或接近最强闭源模型⽔平，编程智能体测试 Terminal-Bench 4.0 中，得分⾼
于 GPT-6 Astra ，仅略低于 Claude Opus 5.5 。⾼速版 Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed 智能与标
准版相当，输出速度约为 2 到 3 倍。标准版 API 输出价 68 元 / 百万 Token ，⾼速版价格为两
倍。⽉之暗⾯在模型开放权重前先做安全评估，模型漏洞研究得分从 90.5% 降⾄ 81% 。
摘要：
中国⼤模型公司⽉之暗⾯推出新⼀代旗舰模型 Kimi K3.1 Pro ，瞄准编程、 3D 创作、⾦融研究等
复杂专业任务，并计划于 11 ⽉ 4 ⽇前开放完整模型权重。这款模型总参数规模达 2.8 万亿，采⽤
混合专家（ MoE ）架构，每次激活 1040 亿参数，⽀持 100 万 Token 上下⽂窗⼝。
⽉之暗⾯称， Kimi K3.1 Pro 是 K3 系列的⾸个模型，也是公司迄今能⼒最强的 3 万亿级参数模

型。与此前侧重通⽤能⼒的模型相⽐，新模型着重强化了⻓程编程、 3D 建模、⾦融分析以及智
能体（ Agent ）执⾏复杂任务的能⼒，并在多项测试中达到或接近顶尖闭源模型的⽔平。
⽉之暗⾯还同步推出⾼速版 Kimi K3.1 Pro Highspeed ，称其智能⽔平与标准版相当，输出速度
可提⾼⾄约 2 ⾄ 3 倍。⽬前，两款模型已通过 Kimi 官⽹、 App 、 Kimi Work 、 Kimi Code 及 API 提供
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

服务，完整模型权重预计于 11 ⽉ 4 ⽇前发布。
对⽉之暗⾯⽽⾔， Kimi K3.1 Pro 此次升级的核⼼，不只是将参数规模推⾄ 3 万亿级别，⽽是进⼀
步把编程、空间推理、⾦融分析和⻓程智能体执⾏能⼒整合到同⼀模型中。
随着完整权重即将开放，市场接下来需要观察的，将是这些基准测试中的优势能否在真实项⽬
中持续兑现，以及开发者能否以可接受的部署成本，将模型能⼒转化为稳定、可靠的⽣产⼒。
⼤规模强化学习加持，复杂任务执⾏能⼒提升
Kimi K3.1 Pro 并⾮从头训练的新模型，⽽是基于 Kimi K3 进⾏后训练的版本。⽉之暗⾯表⽰，新
模型⼤幅增加了后训练算⼒，并围绕强化学习框架、⻓上下⽂推理、训练效率、沙箱基础设施
和任务调度系统等环节进⾏了优化，以⽀持数百个下游领域的⼤规模联合训练。
⽉之暗⾯认为，这⼀训练路线的关键在于，将更⼤规模的强化学习投⼊转化为复杂任务中的持
续执⾏能⼒，⽽不仅仅是提⾼单轮问答的表现。
⽉之暗⾯称，在代码、 Agent 和知识类基准上， K3.1 Pro 达到或接近最强闭源模型的⽔平。其

透露，在编程智能体测试 Terminal-Bench 4.0 中， Kimi K3.1 Pro 获得 65.7% 的评估得分，⾼于
OpenAI 的 GPT-6 Astra 的 58.2% ，与 Anthropic 的 Claude Opus 5.5 的 66.4% 仅有微弱差距。
Terminal-Bench 4.0 测试通过真实环境中的终端任务，考察模型在资源受限条件下使⽤⼯具、执
⾏命令和解决编程问题的能⼒。
在⽉之暗⾯内部的 Kimi Code Bench 3.0 Long-horizon 测试中，新模型得分为 61.2% ，⾼于上⼀
代 K3 的 55.95% 。这项测试侧重多步骤执⾏、⼯具调⽤以及端到端⼯程问题解决能⼒。
需要注意的是，上述成绩来⾃不同的测试体系，其中部分为⽉之暗⾯内部基准。不同模型的测
试环境、⼯具配置和评估⽅法可能存在差异，分数不能直接等同于所有实际⼯作场景中的能⼒
排名。
从编写代码到开发游戏，尝试端到端完成整个项⽬

### Page 3

在编程⽅⾯，⽉之暗⾯希望 Kimi K3.1 Pro 能够处理从修复现有代码库中的问题，到从零开发完
整软件项⽬的任务。
⽉之暗⾯展⽰的案例包括使⽤虚幻引擎（ Unreal Engine ）、 Unity 和 Godot 等游戏引擎开发游
戏，以及⽣成⽹⻚、 Three.js 交互场景和 3D 资产。
其中，在虚幻引擎 5.8 中，模型构建了⼀座位于云海之上的东⽅神话天宫。⽉之暗⾯称，项⽬中
的柱廊通过 Blender 程序化建模，源模型包含 3600 万三⻆形，并使⽤ Nanite 和 Lumen 进⾏渲染。
另⼀个案例是基于 Godot 开发的 3D 动作冒险游戏，包含五个可探索区域、程序化⽣成的地形与
村庄、第三⼈称战⽃、两阶段 Boss 战、昼夜循环和快速传送等功能。
模型还完成了浏览器内的 3D 机械臂展⽰⻚⾯，⽣成 2317 个零件和 268 条动态⾛线，并实现分解
视图、透明外壳和剖⾯展⽰。
这些案例试图说明，模型的⽬标不再局限于⽣成代码⽚段，⽽是进⼀步承担项⽬规划、调⽤专

业⼯具、⽣成素材、调试和迭代等⼯作。
⽉之暗⾯还展⽰了⼀项更复杂的仿真任务：模型基于开源⼈体解剖学模型构建⼈类⾏⾛动⼒学
仿真器，并在 167 项任务、 420 次独⽴评审过程中持续调整⾏⾛控制器，最终让仿真⼈体实现连
续⾏⾛ 11.2 秒。
更值得注意的是，当调整奖励机制⽆法改善⼿臂运动时，模型转⽽检查仿真器动⼒学，定位并
修正了肩部肌⾁⼒臂计算中的 36 处错误，再重新训练控制器。
这类案例体现了⻓程智能体能⼒的⼀个重要⽅向：模型不仅要⽣成解决⽅案，还需要根据实验
反馈定位问题，并在多轮执⾏中持续改进结果。不过，演⽰案例本⾝并不能代表模型在所有同
类任务中都能稳定复现相同表现。
3D 建模能⼒增强，⽀持 Blender 与 CAD 专业⼯具
除编程外， 3D 创作与空间推理也是 Kimi K3.1 Pro 此次升级的重点。
⽉之暗⾯称， Kimi K3.1 Pro 擅⻓ 3D 创作与空间推理，擅⻓参数化建模，例如机械零件、建筑模
型、多参数可配置的产品和定制家具。在 BenchCAD 基准测试中，该模型借助⼯具获得 92.7% 的

成绩，接近 Claude Opus 5.5 的 96.2% 和 GPT-6 Astra 的 95.9% 。
BenchCAD 主要考察模型能否根据多个视⻆的渲染图，通过⽣成 CAD 代码重建 3D 模型。模型需
要理解物体的形状、⽐例和空间关系，再将视觉信息转化为可⽤的三维结构。
⽉之暗⾯表⽰， Kimi K3.1 Pro 能够根据⽂字描述、草图或参考图⽚推断物体结构，并直接编写
和运⾏ Blender 、 CAD 等专业⼯具的脚本，完成参数化建模、⼏何调整、材质修改和场景布局等
⼯作，⽆需依赖 MCP 连接器。

### Page 4

⽉之暗⾯展⽰的案例包括在 Blender 中构建机场室内⾬林场景、使⽤ FreeCAD 和 Python ⽣成包含
2100 个实体的涡扇发动机模型，以及设计可直接进⾏ 3D 打印的台灯。
其中，涡扇发动机模型⽀持分步装配、内部结构展⽰和多个转⼦的旋转动画；台灯则借鉴福建
⼟楼的圆形建筑形态，设计成五个部件组成的镂空结构。
与此同时， Kimi 官⽹和 App 已上线 3D Make 功能，⽀持在线预览和编辑 3D 模型，并允许⽤⼾下
载模型⽤于 3D 打印，或导出视频和实况照⽚。
这意味着⽉之暗⾯正尝试将模型的空间推理和代码⽣成能⼒，进⼀步转化为可直接使⽤的创作
⼯具。
⾦融研究成为重点突破⽅向，事实错误率下降
⾦融研究是 Kimi K3.1 Pro 另⼀项重点强化的能⼒。
⽉之暗⾯表⽰，新模型能够围绕财务建模、投资分析和⾏业研究完成较完整的⼯作流，⽽不仅
仅是回答财务问题或总结研报。
在 OfficeQA Pro v2 基准测试中， Kimi K3.1 Pro 获得 64.4 分，⽉之暗⾯称其达到当前最优⽔平

（ SoTA ）。该测试侧重基于真实⾦融⽂档进⾏复杂数据分析，涉及信息检索、跨⽂档核对和多步
骤数值推理。
在公司内部的财务建模测试中，新模型较 K3 提升 14% ；⾦融研究测试提升 20% ；商业运营测试
的得分则达到 K3 的约 4.2 倍。
在实际应⽤案例中，模型⽣成了英伟达的财务模型，⽀持经营假设调整、公式联动和敏感性分
析；还制作了宁德时代的投资委员会报告，梳理市场⼀致预期与公司实际表现之间的差异，并
整理未来可能的催化因素。
⽉之暗⾯还展⽰了⼀份量⼦计算⾏业研究报告，强调报告中的数据和引⽤均可追溯⾄⼀⼿来
源。
不过，⾦融研究对准确性的要求远⾼于⼀般内容⽣成。模型能够制作完整的财务⼯作簿或研究
报告，并不意味着其中每项数据、假设和结论都必然正确，实际使⽤仍需核验关键数字及其来
源。
针对这⼀问题，⽉之暗⾯专⻔开展了事实准确性压⼒测试。
在内部的 Kimi Hallu Bench Hard 测试中，新模型的单项事实陈述错误率从 K3 的 23.4% 降⾄
8.3% 。包含⾄少⼀处事实错误的回答⽐例，则降⾄前代的约 42.4% 。

⽉之暗⾯特别指出，这项测试刻意选取模型容易出错的⾼难度场景，测试结果不代表⽇常使⽤
中的整体错误率。
尽管如此，降低事实错误率仍是模型进⼊⾦融、企业运营等专业场景的重要条件。对于这些任
务⽽⾔，模型能否提供可追溯的证据、验证计算过程并及时发现错误，可能⽐单纯提⾼回答速
度更为重要。
标准版 API 输出价 68 元 / 百万 Token 开放权重前先做安全评估
在商业化⽅⾯，⽉之暗⾯采取了产品服务先⾏、模型权重随后开放的安排。
⽬前， Kimi K3.1 Pro 已接⼊ Kimi 官⽹、 App 、 Kimi Work 、 Kimi Code 和 Kimi API 。开发者可通
过 API 调⽤模型，企业⽤⼾则可将其⽤于编程、研究和其他智能体⼯作流。
按照⽉之暗⾯公布的价格，标准版 Kimi K3.1 Pro 的 API 输⼊价格为每百万 Token 缓存命中 1.3 元、

缓存未命中 13 元，输出价格为 68 元。⾼速版输出速度约为标准版的 2 ⾄ 3 倍，价格为标准版的两
倍。
⽉之暗⾯还推出了 Kimi Hosted Agents 托管智能体服务，为企业和开发者提供云端运⾏环境，⽀
持⻓时间任务执⾏和多智能体协作，降低⾃⾏搭建和维护智能体运⾏框架的成本。
更受开源社区关注的是模型权重的发布计划。⽉之暗⾯表⽰，完整权重将在 11 ⽉ 4 ⽇前开放。这
将使开发者有机会在⾃⼰的基础设施上部署、研究和适配模型，具体使⽤范围仍需以最终发布
的许可条款为准。
与此同时，⽉之暗⾯也披露了开放权重模型的⽹络安全评估。公司表⽰，在准备开放 Kimi K3.1
Pro 权重时，已从中期训练和强化学习阶段识别并移除了⽹络攻防相关数据，以限制模型的漏洞
研究能⼒。
在⾃研的 xdev-bench-hard 测试中，未经相关数据移除处理的模型得分为 90.5% ，最终版得分为
81.0% 。公司称，这表明针对性的数据清理降低了模型的漏洞研究能⼒，但更强的通⽤编程和⻓
周期调试能⼒仍可能迁移⾄⽹络安全任务。

这也反映出开放权重模型⾯临的两难：⼀⽅⾯，开放模型有助于企业和研究⼈员在本地部署、
检查代码和开展防御性安全⼯作；另⼀⽅⾯，模型能⼒不断增强，也要求开发者评估其在⽹络
攻防等双重⽤途场景中的潜在⻛险。

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research 
– 
26 October 2026

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.5% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4.25% - 4.50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y in Q3 2026 (1.2% q/q) after 4.3% y/y in Q2 and 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a 
whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth remains characterised by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports 
and the fragility of sectors that rely on domestic demand has widened this year. Retail sales barely increased in the first nine months of 2026, 
the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting investment 
and private consumption will increase over the last months of the year . CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.5% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of G erman defence spending on the real economy. Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach  3.1% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), before easing to 3.0% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we 
anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause . That said, 
stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP growth remained low in Q2 (0% after -0.2% in Q1) as a result of deteriorated agricultural and construction output, as well as higher 
inflation (2.5% in 2026 according to our forecast, after 0.9% in 2025) . In 2026, GDP growth should  reach 0.5% (after 0.9% in 2025), driven 
mainly by exports.

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would improve slightly to 1.3% in 2026 and remain at this level in 2027  (1.2% in 2025). The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averag ing 3.2% in 2026 and 3.5% in 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting 
second-round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. 
The BoE is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

Document 365: Top of Mind - Assessing a Less Transparent Fed

Source PDF: all report pdf/Top of Mind - Assessing a Less Transparent Fed.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs (Top of Mind)
Date: 2026-08-25
Pages: 26
Status: ok

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh is steering the Fed into a less transparent era. So, is that
a positive or negative development, and what could it mean for markets and the
economy? We speak with Former Fed governors Donald Kohn and Stephen Miran
as well as GS’ Jan Hatzius who agree that rigid “Odyssean” forward guidance that
commits policymakers to a pre-set path risks policy mistakes and is therefore rarely
appropriate. But they somewhat disagree about the benefits of great er transparency

around the Fed’s reaction function. Hatzius and Kohn argue that such transparency
helps markets better anticipate and price in policy changes, accelerating monetary
policy transmission. But Miran believes that the line between committing to a path
and being transparent is clearer in theory than in practice. And while GS economists
and strategists find that reduc ed transparency leads to increased market volatility,
the question is whether this just increases the market’s noise or also improves the

market’s signal. Hatzius argues largely for the former and Miran for the latter.
Jan Hatzius, Chief Economist and Head of Global Investment Research, Goldman Sachs
“Markets price what they think the Fed will do, not what they think the Fed should do. That will remain true even if the Fed
obscures its reaction function… market guesses about the Fed’s next moves will simply become worse, leading to more
volatility... and importantly, volatility that serves no constructive economic purpose.”

Donald Kohn, former Vice Chairman of the Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System, Robert V . Roosa
Chair in International Economics and Senior Fellow, Brookings Institution
“I sympathize with Chairman Warsh to some extent. [But] the real problem with Warsh’s approach is his reluctance to articulate
how the FOMC sees the economy evolving… explaining the Fed’s thinking about the economic outlook is essential in helping
markets act as a stabilizing force and is a core component of accountability.”

### Page 3

El
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 3 
Top of Mind Issue 151

New Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh is steering the Fed into a less 
transparent era characterized by shorter post-meeting 
statements, less forward guidance, and a more limited role for 
projections, marking a meaningful break from the 
transparency revolution that has defined central banking over 
the last quarter century. So, what could a less transparent Fed 
mean for markets and the economy? 
For answers, we turn to former Fed governors Donald Kohn, 
now Robert V. Roosa Chair in International Economics and 
senior fellow at the Brookings Institution, and Stephen Miran, 
now senior strategist at Hudson Bay Capital Management, as 
well as Jan Hatzius, GS’ Chief Economist and Head of Global 
Investment Research. 
We first ask whether scaling back Fed communication is a 
positive or negative development. Hatzius says it’s likely 
negative, though he draws a clear line between transparency 
and forward guidance. He argues that clarity about the Fed’s 
reaction function helps markets better anticipate policy 
changes and quickly incorporate new information into financial 
conditions, thereby accelerating monetary policy transmission 
to the economy. So, he believes that unwinding that 
transparency would be a mistake. (Note: GS Global 
Economists Joseph Briggs and Megan Peters lay out the 
evidence backing this argument on pgs. 14-15.) 
But Hatzius takes a more nuanced view of forward guidance, 
arguing that rigid “Odyssean” guidance that commits 
policymakers to a pre-set path regardless of new information 
only makes sense at the effective lower bound for the policy 
rate, while conditional “Delphic” guidance tied to the 
economic outlook is generally more defensible. Although 
markets could confuse the two, he believes that most 
sophisticated investors understand the difference and is 
therefore not particularly worried that markets have become 
overly reliant on the policy dots published in the Fed’s 
Summary of Economic Projections (SEP). Instead, he 
considers them a “useful source of additional information 
about the Fed’s reaction function” (except for the long-run 
policy dot). If anything, Hatzius would prefer the dots move 
in an even more informative direction—by publishing each 
policymaker’s combined projections for growth, inflation, 
unemployment, and the policy rate on an anonymized basis. 
Kohn agrees that clarity about the Fed’s “narrative” and 
reaction function is vital. Explaining how the Fed sees the 
economy evolving and how policy would respond, he says, is 
“essential in helping markets act as a stabilizing force and is 
a core component of accountability.” Still, he sympathizes 
somewhat with Chairman Warsh’s view that the dot plot is 
“not helpful” (see pg. 9 for Warsh’s own words on this), 
arguing that the market’s hyper-focus on the SEP’s median 
policy dot “illustrates the risk of excessive precision and 
false certainty,” as small shifts in it can trigger unwarranted 
market moves. So, he has long favored omitting the median. 
Miran, though, is more supportive of Warsh’s push for a less 
transparent Fed. Like Hatzius and Kohn, he’s concerned that 
excessive forward guidance that locks the Fed into a policy 
path raises the risk of policy errors and also argues that such 
guidance can impair market pricing of risk. But he’s less 
convinced about the benefits of increased transparency 
around the Fed’s reaction function, making the case that the 
distinction between the Fed committing to a pre-set policy 
path and being transparent about its reaction function is clear 
in theory, but less so in practice. So, he thinks “the policy dots 
absolutely have to go” but feels less strongly about omitting 
the SEP’s economic projections. 
But would less transparency allow markets to provide the Fed 
with more “direct and unfiltered” information about the 
economy, as Warsh has argued? The answer seems to boil 
down to the implications for market volatility, and whether any 
resulting volatility would simply increase the market’s noise or 
also improve its signal. 
As for the volatility implications, the evidence is fairly clear. 
Briggs and Peters find that increased central bank 
transparency has led to a reduction in market volatility over the 
last several decades. This is consistent with GS Head of US 
Rates Strategy William Marshall’s recent observation of the 
reverse: that the US rates market has already priced 
substantial uncertainty associated with a less transparent Fed, 
with the July FOMC representing the largest non-cut meeting 
day surprise in almost three decades.  
But Marshall argues that whether such meeting-day surprises 
lead to structurally higher rates volatility depends (again) on 
whether the Fed’s reaction function is well understood. If it’s 
not, he would expect higher rates volatility to persist. GS FX 
Strategists Michael Cahill and Lexi Kanter find a similar 
pattern in FX: that central bank communication shifts can 
change which events and data drive FX volatility without 
necessarily raising it, but less transparency could increase 
Dollar volatility—all else equal (though global macro 
conditions remain its biggest driver).  
But the key debate seems to be whether the increased 
volatility resulting from less Fed transparency will improve or 
worsen the market’s signal. Hatzius is clearly in the “worsen” 
camp. He argues that markets price what they think the Fed 
will do—not what it should do—and less transparency won’t 
change that; it would simply produce worse guesses. So, he 
says that while volatility in and of itself is not necessarily bad, 
volatility driven by uncertainty about the Fed’s reaction 
function would serve no constructive economic purpose. 
Kohn agrees that the lack of a clear Fed narrative could create 
damaging uncertainty. But he argues that removing overly 
specific guidance could help free markets to process new 
information more independently and isn’t too worried about 
modestly higher volatility as a result. And Miran seems most 
convinced that reducing transparency would improve the 
markets’ signal, with volatility an unavoidable but acceptable 
cost: “The first best world of signal without volatility doesn't 
exist. I'd rather have that signal, even if it's messy, than 
throw the signal out by anchoring market expectations to the 
Fed's near-term projections.” 
Allison Nathan, Editor

Assessing less Fed transparency

### Page 4

El
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 4 
Top of Mind Issue 151

Jan Hatzius is Chief Economist and Head of Global Investment Research at Goldman Sachs. 
Below, he argues that a shift toward a less transparent Fed is likely a negative development.  
Ashley Rhodes: Fed Chairman Kevin 
Warsh appears to be ushering the 
Fed into a less transparent era. Is 
that shift a positive or negative 
development? 
Jan Hatzius: It’s likely a negative 
development, although I would 
distinguish between transparency and 
forward guidance. The transparency 
revolution in central banking over the last 30 years has been 
extremely successful. When a central bank provides 
meaningful information about its reaction function—how it’s 
likely to respond to different economic outcomes—markets can 
better anticipate policy changes. So, new information gets 
incorporated quickly into financial conditions, accelerating the 
transmission of monetary policy. The central bank can later 
decide whether to ratify that move and always retains the 
option not to. That framework has worked well for the Fed over 
the past several decades. Unwinding transparency about the 
reaction function risks delaying the transmission of monetary 
policy and would be a mistake, in my view. 
Forward guidance about the policy rate path is a separate and 
more nuanced issue. In assessing the pros and cons, it’s 
important to distinguish between “Odyssean” and “Delphic” 
forward guidance. With Odyssean guidance, like Odysseus 
tying himself to the mast, the central bank commits to a policy 
path even if subsequent data point in a different direction. That 
only makes sense at the effective lower bound for the policy 
rate, when conventional monetary easing is no longer an 
option. Even then, the hurdle for Odyssean guidance should be 
high because there is a real risk that policymakers lock 
themselves into an inappropriate policy stance. By contrast, 
Delphic guidance, named for the Oracle of Delphi, is a 
conditional description of what policymakers expect to do given 
their economic outlook that will change with new information. 
Delphic guidance is more defensible, even in normal times. 
Ashley Rhodes: Let’s take these in turn. Do you agree with 
the argument that Odyssean forward guidance increases 
the risk of policy errors? 
Jan Hatzius: That argument is a serious one. In terms of 
practical examples, it is heavily centered on the 2021–2022 
episode, when the Fed tightened policy much too late because 
they made a major forecasting error on inflation at a time of 
rigid Odyssean forward guidance. That was a bad combination. 
If the forecast is wrong, policy will be set incorrectly under any 
communication or transparency framework. And Odyssean 
forward guidance compounds that problem because it delays 
the inevitable course correction. This is why Odyssean forward 
guidance should be used sparingly, if at all. 
That said, I am not sure how big a difference to the inflation 
outcome or to economic performance more broadly it would 
have made if the Fed had started to tighten in, say, September 
2021 rather than March 2022. By early 2022, markets had 
already recognized that the funds rate would need to rise, and 
financial conditions tightened substantially on the back of 
higher rate expectations well before the Fed delivered those 
hikes. Also, the error was ultimately not disastrous 
economically; inflation expectations did not de-anchor and the 
Fed course-corrected without causing a recession. 
Ashley Rhodes: Even if the Fed just provides Delphic 
guidance, are concerns that the market might confuse it 
with Odyssean guidance warranted, especially given how 
much weight markets put on the dot plot? 
Jan Hatzius: Such confusion would be problematic, but I think 
it doesn’t happen frequently in practice. Most sophisticated 
observers understand that the policy dots included in the 
Summary of Economic Projections (SEP) are conditional on data 
evolving in line with the economic projections. And the dots 
only provide information about the individual and anonymized 
views of FOMC participants, including non-voters. So, they 
aren’t strong forward guidance, not even strong Delphic 
forward guidance. And market expectations have often 
diverged meaningfully from them, undermining the argument 
that investors view them as a commitment.  
Instead, the dots are a useful source of additional information 
about the Fed’s reaction function. The SEP could be made even 
more informative by publishing each policymaker’s combined 
projections for growth, inflation, unemployment, and the Fed 
funds rate on an anonymous basis, which we have long argued 
for. Doing so would provide a clearer picture of how 
policymakers’ economic forecasts relate to their anticipated 
policy paths. But that seems unlikely given Warsh’s apparent 
preference for moving in the opposite direction. 
Concerns about market confusion are more understandable 
when it comes to the long-run policy dot, where the Fed has 
less information to convey. Nobody knows where R-star is—as 
Chair Powell liked to say, “it is seen by its works”—but 
markets seem to assume that the Fed has special knowledge 
and therefore place too much weight on the long-term dot. 
That can lock down longer-term rate expectations too firmly. 
That said, removing the long-run dot could complicate the Fed’s 
communication in its current form, as it has become an 
important benchmark for assessing the stance of monetary 
policy. I would prefer to eliminate the long-run dot and 
communicate the stance of policy with greater emphasis on 
financial conditions. Overall, though, the dot plot works well, 
and, despite the controversy surrounding it, I remain a fan. 
Ashley Rhodes: Aren’t the Fed’s inflation and employment 
targets sufficient for the market to infer the Fed’s reaction? 
Jan Hatzius: No, because getting from point A to point B 
requires more information than just a stated objective. Take the 
inflation target, for example. The Fed's goal is 2% inflation, but 
how do you assess whether inflation is moving sustainably 
toward that target? How do you determine the current 
underlying rate of inflation? Should you look at headline PCE, 
core PCE, trimmed mean inflation, or something else entirely? 
Do you project future inflation changes based on the 
Interview with Jan Hatzius

### Page 5

El
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 5
Top of Mind Issue 151
unemployment rate via a Phillips curve relationship, the
monetary aggregates, or something else? To anticipate the
Fed’s moves—and thereby push financial conditions in a
stabilizing direction—markets need insight into how the Fed
assesses these questions. Those considerations go well
beyond stating a broad policy goal.
Ashley Rhodes: Is there any merit to communicating less
given that Fed officials have, at times, inadvertently made

market-moving remarks?
Jan Hatzius: There is certainly an optimal level and frequency
of communication. The Fed should not provide running
commentary on every data release, especially as markets tend
to overemphasize individual data points relative to the longer-
term trend. The Reserve Bank presidents speak a lot more
frequently than the governors and their comments are almost
by definition less informative about the policy path than those
of the Fed leadership. While that can complicate the public's

understanding of the policy outlook, it can also be informative
for market participants who nevertheless understand that the
views of the troika—the Fed chairman, Fed vice chair, and New
York Fed president—carry the greatest weight. On net, it’s
therefore unclear whether noise from frequent policymaker
comments has had big practical costs. More broadly,
communication mistakes are inevitable, and policymakers will
say things they later wish they hadn't. But the question is

whether these occasional mistakes outweigh the benefits from
transparent communication. In my view, they don’t.
Ashley Rhodes: Warsh believes that providing less
guidance about the Fed’s reaction function will allow
policymakers to draw more “direct and unfiltered”
information from markets. So, you don’t agree?
Jan Hatzius: I don’t. Markets price what they think the Fed will
do, not what they think the Fed should do. That will remain true
even if the Fed obscures its reaction function. So, if the Fed

chooses to do so, market guesses about the Fed’s next moves
will simply become worse, leading to more volatility in rates
markets and in financial conditions—and importantly, volatility
that serves no constructive economic purpose. Volatility is not
bad in and of itself. For example, if new data shows increased
inflation risks, financial conditions should tighten as markets
price in rate hikes. This accelerates the transmission of
monetary policy. But volatility driven by uncertainty about the

Fed’s reaction function is more random and doesn’t necessarily
push financial conditions in a stabilizing direction.
Also, policymakers can already extract valuable information
from market indicators. For example, a sharp rise in breakeven
inflation above target is a statement that markets view the
expected policy path as inappropriately loose. So, the
information is already available without the Fed saying less.
Ashley Rhodes: Is there a risk of a “hall of mirrors” effect if

the Fed uses market prices to not only glean information
about the economy but also to guide what it should do?
Jan Hatzius: Yes, but I am not particularly concerned about
this risk. While a “hall of mirrors” effect—whereby markets
watch the Fed for clues, but the Fed watches the markets for
clues—could push the policy stance far away from desirable
levels, the likelihood of that scenario materializing is low.
Warsh has said that he does not want to “be constrained or

take verbatim from what the market’s doing.” And Fed
officials, including the Chairman, are unlikely to take that logic
to its extreme conclusion and follow the market simply
because it has priced in a particular policy move.
Ashley Rhodes: Warsh has also said that financial markets
“are probably the most important source of information to
guide central bankers.” Do you agree?
Jan Hatzius: Not entirely, as I would rank the economic data
first. That said, financial market prices are very important, and

central banks have become increasingly sophisticated in
extracting information from them. There’s always room for
improvement, but again, the information is already available
under the current regime. And communicating less
transparently wouldn’t help policymakers evaluate financial
market signals more appropriately.
Ashley Rhodes: How durable is a less transparent Fed?
Jan Hatzius: That remains an open question. As Chairman,
Warsh has significant control over the Fed’s communications

framework and can choose to provide less information at press
conferences, as he has done. In that sense, a less transparent
approach could prove durable, though its consequences remain
to be seen. Other communication changes, such as eliminating
the dot plot, would probably in practice require FOMC support,
and my sense is that other Fed officials would see that as too
great a step away from transparency.
A large reduction in Fed communication, defined by the

number of speeches, is less likely to endure. Reserve Bank
presidents have strong incentives to continue talking, in part
because the influence and relevance of their institutions
depend on it. This has been a perennial issue—Chairman Alan
Greenspan was often very frustrated with the Bank presidents.
Warsh could try to rein them in. But even Greenspan in his
heyday failed to do so. If regional bank presidents continue
speaking frequently while centralized communication declines,

the cacophony could actually worsen.
Ashley Rhodes: More broadly, while the Fed held rates
steady in July, three FOMC participants dissented in favor
of a hike, fueling debate about whether further tightening
is on the horizon. Where do you stand?
Jan Hatzius: We don’t expect the Fed to hike, largely because
we think much of the recent strength in core PCE inflation
reflects temporary factors: higher oil prices related to the US-
Iran war, roughly 70bp of lingering tariff pass-through, and

distortions in software and accessories prices. As these effects
fade, we expect core inflation to move close to 2% in 2027.
That assessment is consistent with the ongoing deceleration in
other measures of underlying inflation such as trimmed-mean
PCE and core CPI.
The recent data are also broadly consistent with that view. The
inflation data improved significantly in June and July, and the
labor market reports for both months were somewhat weaker

on balance. If those trends continue, policymakers will likely
conclude that additional tightening is unnecessary, especially
with the funds rate already around 3.5-3.75%—a level the Fed
likely views as roughly neutral or even slightly restrictive.
